# PIX Football QA audit - v0.5.0 playtest (finder: audit lead)

**Running count: 1054 distinct issues logged** (IDs QA-001 .. QA-1054). Status column starts as `open`; fix tracks record the fix in their own files as `QA-123 -> fixed (see ANI-045)`.

## Summary

| Track | Meaning | Count |
|---|---|---|
| ANI | Animation / mocap | 126 |
| CAM | Cameras | 33 |
| GFX | Graphics / lighting | 109 |
| MDL | Models / skeletons / helmets | 74 |
| SIM | Match engine / rules / stats | 313 |
| STN | Stances, facing, formations | 68 |
| UI | UI, flow, settings, audio, text, data, docs | 331 |

| Severity | Meaning | Count |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | broken / very visible | 51 |
| S2 | clearly wrong | 497 |
| S3 | polish | 506 |

| Track | S1 | S2 | S3 | Total |

## How this audit was produced
- Shipped build `Builds/PIXFootball_v0.5.0_Windows` run with `-pixAutotest` flags at 2560x1440 (`Logs/qa_audit/run1` .. `run4_v06look`) plus every older capture set (`Logs/autotest*`, `Docs/Screens`); every screenshot was inspected and each frame produced multiple rows. The autotest hard-codes BUF vs MIA, seed 20261021, so team/rule-set coverage is limited to that pairing plus the scripted college segment (hash, overtime).
- Whole-source code review of `Assets/_Project/Scripts` (Sim, Core, League, Gameplay, UI, Input, Presentation, Editor) and the asset pipeline/ProjectSettings/data/docs by dedicated reviewers. Code rows were found by reading, not reproduced in play, unless the Where column cites a screenshot or log.
- All-Pro Football 2K8 comparison rows use `Docs/QA/REFERENCE_AllPro2K8.md` and `Docs/Reference/AllPro2K8` (rows tagged 2K8 in Where).

## Top 20 most visible / most important issues

| # | ID | Track | What the player sees |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | QA-898 / QA-1031 | ANI/STN | Root cause of backwards OL/DL/LB/CB: clips are imported with each mocap actor's yaw baked in (clip_facing.csv shows DlineIdle3ptR +133, LB blitz idles -153); the runtime facing table is keyed by clip name so cleaned clips get the offset twice |
| 2 | QA-899 / QA-900 / QA-901 | STN | Every OL, C, TE, DL uses one DL-idle clip; every WR/CB/S/returner one single safety clip; LBs use blitz-idle clips - no real per-position stance |
| 3 | QA-306 | STN | Sim has one pre-snap action (Stance) for all 22 players: no 3-point/4-point/2-point/under-center/shotgun state |
| 4 | QA-018 / QA-226 / QA-142 | STN | Pre-snap OL/DL are a stacked column bent at the waist or standing upright, rotated sideways to the line (see run3/match_presnap_1.png, bc_gallery_combined.png) |
| 5 | QA-102 / QA-933 / QA-932 / QA-959 | MDL | Helmet back patch: wrong texture set on the helmet shell (New_Helmet maps on Basic Helmet 1), one material on every slot, data maps imported sRGB; visible white blotch in every close-up (QA-037, QA-071, QA-082, QA-148) |
| 6 | QA-108 / QA-147 / QA-097 | MDL | Real-brand text still on the models: DALLAS COWBOYS on jersey back/chest, DRAFT FEVER BOWL on the chain-gang box |
| 7 | QA-940 | MDL | Thin waists/legs measured: Large LOD1-3 are -18 percent waist, -21 percent hip, -12 percent thigh vs LOD0 (hip narrower than waist); players use LOD2 at broadcast distance |
| 8 | QA-149 | MDL | Every player is #10, number embossed tone-on-tone, no name bar |
| 9 | QA-283 | CAM | Default camera is a high sideline broadcast view (60 px players) - not the behind-QB 2K8/Madden camera |
| 10 | QA-202 | CAM | First-person view dominated by the player's own forearm/helmet mesh |
| 11 | QA-180 | SIM | Three autopilot games: 6-0, 6-0, 6-7, 24-29 snaps, tiny yardage, 0 interceptions and 0 fumbles |
| 12 | QA-415 | SIM | Off-man corners aim behind the receiver and charge the line at the snap |
| 13 | QA-454 | SIM | Interior OL/DL lock at the snap with no first step (contact range larger than the alignment gap) |
| 14 | QA-510 | SIM | I-Form Slot (20 personnel) has 6 on the line (illegal formation); 20 plays inherit it; Playbook.Validate never checks legality |
| 15 | QA-556 | SIM | College players built with the pro age curve: average OVR 43.9, QBs 41 |
| 16 | QA-048 / QA-175 / QA-223 | STN | PAT/FG and kickoff formations are single files of upright players, no kicker/holder/snapper |
| 17 | QA-187 / QA-188 / QA-902 / QA-903 | ANI | Clip library: stance keys map to creep/point gesture clips; Clean folder never produced; 8 keys missing (walk, kick_off, kick_field_goal, tackle_gang, ...) |
| 18 | QA-971 / QA-972 / QA-958 | GFX | Night is flat/overexposed (~10,000 lux, indirect x5); jersey micro-normals imported as colour |
| 19 | QA-812 | GFX | Graphics quality only switches features off; choosing a higher tier never re-enables them |
| 20 | QA-905 | ANI | Slim build whitelist lacks every v0.6+ animation key, so new clips would be missing from shipped builds |

Row format: `| ID | Status | Sev | Track | Category | Where | Issue | Fix | Verified by |`

| QA-306 | open | S1 | STN | Stances/Facing | SimAgent.cs:37,49 + PlaySimulation.cs:242-243,356-357 | The sim only has one pre-snap pose, AgentAction.Stance, for all 22 players; there is no per-position stance (3-point OL/DL, 4-point interior DL/short yardage, 2-point WR/TE/CB/LB, upright S/QB, QB under center vs shotgun). The presentation layer therefore cannot tell an OL from a CB and everybody gets the same crouch, which matches the "wrong stances" complaint. | Add an enum PreSnapStance (ThreePoint, FourPoint, TwoPoint, Upright, UnderCenter, Shotgun, Kneel) to SimAgent, derive it from PlayPos/formation slot in MakeAgent/LayoutOffense/LayoutDefense, and expose it so the animator picks the matching stance clip. |  |
| QA-307 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | PlaySimulation.cs:177-178,238 | Every offensive agent is created with Facing (0,1) and every defender with (0,-1) regardless of role; nothing ever aims a CB at his receiver, an LB at the ball, a returner at the kicker or a gunner at the returner. The kickoff comment at :262 (kicking team faces downfield, receiving team faces the kicker) has no code behind it. Players stand square to the goal line axis and only change heading when an AI first sets HasFace. | After layout, run a PreSnapFacing pass: OL/DL toward the ball, CB toward CoverTarget, safeties/LBs toward the QB/ball, returners toward the kicker, punt/FG blockers toward the line, gunners toward the returner. |  |
| QA-308 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | PlaySimulation.cs:352-360 | The pre-snap Step never sets HasFace, so a player bumped by SeparateBodies or the motion man keeps whatever heading RotateToward gave him and is never re-aimed at the line. A motion man runs sideways facing the sideline (faceTarget = wantDir) instead of keeping his shoulders to the LOS. | In the pre-snap loop set HasFace=true with FaceDir toward the ball for linemen and toward the LOS/QB for the motion man (shuffle facing). |  |
| QA-367 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | OffenseAI.cs:292-294 | While running a route a.HasFace is never set, so the body faces the travel direction; on a curl/comeback/hitch the receiver turns his back to the QB the whole way, and on a hitch snaps 180 degrees; there is no head-snap to the QB or ball before the break and no plant-foot facing on breaks. | Set FaceDir toward the next waypoint plus a look-to-QB window during the last 0.3 s before the break; face the QB after hitch/comeback cuts. |  |
| QA-372 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | OffenseAI.Qb.cs:164-165 | In QbFake the fallback facing (when there is no fake target) is (0,-1), i.e. the quarterback faces his own end zone for 0.32-0.45 s (play action/boot with no CarryAssignment IsFake) and then spins back to (0,1); under the new animator this reads as a QB turning his back on the line. | Fall back to (0,1) and/or the nearest back; never face directly away from the LOS without a handoff target. |  |
| QA-424 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | DefenseAI.cs:522-532 | Zone facing: a deep-zone defender with a target gets HasFace = (dist2 < 4) toward the QB, otherwise no explicit facing; a safety running with the receiver is not turned to the receiver or the ball (face toward QB only), and DBs that backpedal at a receiver 20 yards upfield have their back to him once HasFace is false. | Face the receiver/ball while the DB is within 6 yards; face run direction otherwise; never face the QB when the receiver is behind him. |  |
| QA-452 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | SpecialTeamsAI.cs:306-331 | Before the ball is kicked ReturnerUpdate sets Want=0 without setting HasFace, so deep returners keep their spawn heading (0,-1 from MakeAgent) and rotate only when the ball is kicked; they never turn to watch the ball in the 1 s before the kick, and the backup returner (L314-318) never turns to the ball at all (Want=0 without HasFace) while the ball flies over him. | Set HasFace toward the kicker before the kick and toward the ball while it is in flight for both returners. |  |
| QA-455 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | DefaultPlaybooks.Offense.cs:279-286 + AgentBody.cs:93 | Offensive linemen are placed at Y=0 (their body centre on the line of scrimmage) with a radius up to 0.46, so their front half is 0.46 yd inside the neutral zone (OL must have their heads behind the ball). DL at Y=1.0-1.2 are therefore only ~0.3 yd from the OL front, causing the instant lock above and the OL looking offside. | Place the OL at Y = -0.5 and DL at Y = +0.5 relative to the ball length, or offset by the body radius. |  |
| QA-456 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | PlaySimulation.cs:248-261 + PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:174-215 | Defensive alignment is a fixed offset of the formation relative to the ball; it is never adjusted to the offense's actual set (trips, bunch, empty, 12/22 personnel, flipped plays, motion). A CB at X=-17 stays on the left sideline although the only receivers are bunched right, and base-4-3 corners stay head-up on X=+-17 while the offense has Z at 18 and bunch at 13-16. MatchMen then runs one corner across the formation to a TE/RB. | Add a defensive alignment pass that shifts CBs/safeties/LBs to the offense's strength and receiver splits before the snap. |  |
| QA-628 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | BlockEngagement.cs:569-570 | Engaged pairs are given HasFace toward each other (FaceDir = +-n), but before engagement DefenseAI/BlockerAI give no HasFace to non-engaged linemen at the snap (UpdateBlocker's RunBlockMove sets it only after a target exists), so OL and DL begin the play facing the previous heading and rotate at turn-rate (6.5-12 rad/s) instead of already being square; combined with default heading (0,1) for all defenders is what shows as OL/DL "backwards". | Initialize facing toward the ball at layout time and set HasFace in the first snap frame for every lineman. |  |
| QA-636 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | SpecialTeamsAI.cs:471,509,396 | Holder, snapper and FG kicker are forced to FaceDir (0,1) every tick; the holder (7 yards behind the LOS) should face the LOS but the kicker should also face the holder's spot. More importantly the snapper has Intent=Stance but is also in the OL group, so he faces +Y while an actual FG block unit (the defense) has HasFace unset and never lines up facing the LOS before the snap. | Set explicit pre-snap facing for FG/punt block units toward the ball. |  |
| QA-643 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/SimAgentView.cs:116-120,195 (Player/PlayerAvatar.cs:135-138, Art/ClipFacingTable.cs:53-65) | The new clip-facing fix applies ONE yaw offset as a rotation of the whole Body root (SetVisualYaw(_clipYaw * Rig.Weight)) and switches _clipYaw instantly when a new plan starts. During every crossfade the locomotion pose that is fading in/out is rotated too: at the snap a linemen whose stance clip needs ~130 degrees turns its body through a visible spin while the 0.18 s fade runs, and clip-to-clip changes with different offsets (stance -> block, block -> shed) pop the body by up to 180 degrees in one frame. The pose is only right while exactly one corrected clip is at weight 1. | Fix the clips instead of the root: set a per-clip Rotation Offset (ModelImporterClipAnimation.rotationOffset) from the ClipFacingTable at import so every clip faces +Z natively and delete the runtime yaw; if a runtime fix must stay, rotate only the hips bone in LateUpdate weighted per slot (slot weight x slot offset) rather than the whole Body. |  |
| QA-644 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/SimAgentView.cs:119 (Match/PlayStage.cs PlayReaction, Contact/ContactDirector.cs Rig.Play calls) | ApplyClipFacing forces the correction to 0 whenever ContactDriven or Ragdoll is active, and PlayReaction/ContactDirector call Rig.Play directly without setting _clipYaw, so cheer/frustration reactions, get-up takes and pair takes never get the ClipFacingTable offset: a reaction clip authored facing 180 degrees plays with the player's back to the camera. | Look the offset up in one place (ActionAnimator.Play) so every clip, whoever plays it, is corrected. |  |
| QA-645 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/SimAgentView.cs:195 (Art/ClipFacingTable.cs:5-12,28-38,57) | The correction depends on a git-ignored Resources asset (PixClipFacing) that must be rebuilt after every re-import; if it is missing Offset() silently returns 0 (every stance faces backwards again, no log) and a stale table double-corrects (its own comment). There is no runtime or autotest check that the corrected pose really faces the sim. | Log once when the table is missing/older than the clips and add a pre-snap autotest that compares BodyHeading.Measure(animator).Yaw against the sim facing for all 22 views. |  |
| QA-649 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/StanceRules.cs:133 | CenterSnap, ThreePoint and FourPoint all return the single key dl_stance, so the centre, every OL, every DL, the TE on the line and the goal-line 4-point interior are the same clip pool with only hand-down scoring; the OL never uses ol_stance and FourPoint never uses stance_4pt/stance_dline although those keys exist in ArtKeys. StanceKind changes nothing the viewer sees for the line (note 6). | Return distinct keys per kind (ol_stance for OL, stance_4pt for FourPoint, a snap clip for CenterSnap) and fall back to dl_stance only if a key has no clips. |  |
| QA-650 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/StanceRules.cs:139-140 | LinebackerTwoPoint plays idle_alt (the library rule mixes receivertransitionreadyidle, linebackerblitzidle and 2pstance_halfback) and CornerPress, CornerOff and SafetyDeep all return the single key stance_safety with no handedness scoring (PickByHandedness only scores line and receiver kinds): press corners, off corners and safeties are visually identical and backers randomly look like receivers or halfbacks. | Add lb_stance, cb_press, cb_off and safety_deep keys (or at least separate clip pools per kind) and score variants by Mirror/foot-back for DBs too. |  |
| QA-651 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/StanceRules.cs:134-138 | WR/slot/gunner/kickoff/returner all use stance_2pt, whose rule is the catch-all ^2pstance_ (ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:58) that includes the safety and halfback stance takes; RB and FB use stance_halfback for every back (a FB lined up in the I-form is BackTwoPoint at L111 unless exactly 2.5-4.5 yards deep). Receivers can show a safety or back pose and every back is the same. | Split stance_2pt into wr/te/slot/returner pools by clip name and give FB/RB separate keys. |  |
| QA-652 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Player/StanceRules.cs:10,26-28,141 | Kicker, holder (FieldGoal QB), P and anything mapped to Upright get no stance at all (the comment promises clips 'until they exist'), and KickoffRunner/Returner (the whole kickoff and punt coverage and return units, 20 of 22 players) use the same 2pt pool: on special teams nobody has a role-specific stance (long snapper, wings, wedge blockers). | Add snapper, wing, gunner and wedge stances and a kicker approach pose; log when a kind falls back to Upright. |  |
| QA-653 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Match/PlayStage.cs:108 (Sim/PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:36-47, 190) | v.Stance is computed once in Begin from StartPos, but flip, audible, motion and the defensive Press/Off/Line/Backer shifts rewrite positions afterwards (StartPos is rewritten too): handedness (Mirror/IsLeftOfBall) stays on the old side after a flip and CornerPress/CornerOff keeps the old depth after the user toggles press coverage. | Recompute v.Stance for all views after every successful pre-snap change (Sim fires a PreSnapChanged event). |  |
| QA-671 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Match/PlayFrame.cs:44 (Player/SimAgentView.cs:19) | Degenerate-direction fallbacks disagree: PlayFrame.DirToUnity returns Vector3.forward (+Z = BACKWARDS for a -Z attacking frame, ignores _s) while SimSpace.DirToUnity returns Vector3.zero and ApplyMatch then does Atan2(0,0)=0. A zero sim facing turns the player to world +Z whatever the attack direction. | Return the frame's downfield direction and skip the yaw update on a zero vector. |  |
| QA-672 | open | S2 | STN | Stances/Facing | Sim/PlaySimulation.cs:355-357 (Sim/AgentBody.cs:178-180) | During pre-snap motion Body.Step is called with hasFace=false and a lateral wantVel, so facing follows the motion direction: the man in motion turns 90 degrees to the sideline and plays Run instead of staying square to the line, then re-turns at the snap. | Call Body.Step with hasFace=true and FaceDir=(0,1) during motion and drive the lateral shuffle through the strafe/shuffle blend. |  |
| QA-673 | open | S3 | STN | Stances/Facing | Gameplay/Match/MatchDirector.PreSnap.cs:68,190 (Sim/PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:36-47) | Flip, audible and defensive line/backer shifts rewrite Body.Pos directly (LayoutOffense/LayoutDefense) and the view follows the same frame: linemen and backers teleport sideways without walking or re-settling. | Move agents with a short pre-snap walk to the new slot and re-trigger the stance after arrival. |  |

| QA-013 | open | S2 | SIM | Performance | autotest.txt free roam FPS | Free-roam segment min frame 3.8 FPS (a ~260 ms hitch) with 1% low 85.6; a hitch at team-select -> stadium load/first sprint. | Pre-warm shaders/PSOs and the stadium on scene load; find the allocation spike (GC.Alloc in first moves/IK init). |  |
| QA-678 | open | S3 | ANI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/PlayStage.cs:83 (MatchDirector.cs:123) | Warm(8,8,9) pre-creates 8 small bodies but nickel/dime sets need up to 9-10 small (WR, CB, S, K), goal-line/jumbo sets 10 large, and the toss now takes 4 more medium bodies, so avatars (prefab instantiate, materials, ragdoll, PlayableGraph) are created mid-game and hitch. | Warm to the worst case (10 small, 11 medium, 11 large). |  |
| QA-682 | open | S2 | CAM | Performance | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:237-238 (Core/PixSettings.cs:21-31) | UpdateProxies reads PixSettings.UserCameraStyle and four camera trims every LateUpdate: five PlayerPrefs lookups plus string concatenation (K + "camh" etc.) per frame, and the style struct is rebuilt every frame. | Cache style and trims in fields refreshed by a SettingsChanged event. |  |
| QA-694 | open | S3 | ANI | Performance | Gameplay/Contact/RagdollRig.cs:160-193 | The ragdoll is built lazily on the first hit: ~33 AddComponent calls (Rigidbody, collider, CharacterJoint) per player in one frame; with 22 pooled players the first big hits hitch. | Build the rigs during Stage.Warm (or on the call screen) with colliders disabled. |  |
| QA-709 | open | S2 | GFX | Performance | Gameplay/Look/WeatherSystem.cs:301-308,115 | PushGlobals runs EVERY LateUpdate and calls Flag() three times (-pixNoWetShader twice, -pixNoPuddles), each calling Environment.GetCommandLineArgs() (allocates a new string[]), plus a string-keyed Shader.SetGlobalFloat("_PixLegacy"): several allocations per frame forever. | Cache the flags in static readonly bools and use Shader.PropertyToID once. |  |
| QA-710 | open | S3 | GFX | Performance | Gameplay/Core/PixSettings.cs:12-60 (Match/MatchAudio.cs:94-95, World/StadiumAudio.cs:127) | Every PixSettings getter builds its key with `K + "..."` and reads PlayerPrefs; MatchAudio.Update and StadiumAudio.Update read CrowdVolume (string concat plus PlayerPrefs lookup) every frame, and QualityTiers.Current reads Quality on each property use. | Cache the values and refresh on a settings-changed event. |  |
| QA-711 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/MatchInput.cs:65 | Poll() allocates three `new bool[6]` arrays every frame in every phase. | Reuse member arrays and clear them. |  |
| QA-712 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/MatchDirector.cs:1089-1101,1163 (MatchDirector.PreSnap.cs:42-46) | UpdateIcons builds three new Lists, a closure sort and a Btn[] every frame while the QB holds the ball; ReceiverSlots builds a List plus lambda sort every frame in HotPick/Motion modes and again in the HUD update; AutoInput allocates `new[] {7,8,9,10,1}` per frame. | Use cached lists/arrays, a static comparison and pre-allocated button arrays. |  |
| QA-719 | open | S3 | GFX | Performance | Gameplay/Look/FieldWear.cs:104-113 | Flush dispatches the compute shader once per queued stamp (up to 512 per frame, each with SetInts/SetVector). | Upload the stamps in one ComputeBuffer and dispatch once. |  |
| QA-725 | open | S3 | ANI | Performance | Gameplay/Player/BodyMotion.cs:115,147-150,161-167 | GetBoneTransform is called 3-8 times per player per frame in Late/ApplyProtect/ApplyArm (native calls x 22 players) instead of caching the bones. | Cache Spine, Chest, Head and arm bones in the constructor. |  |
| QA-727 | open | S3 | ANI | Performance | Gameplay/Player/LocomotionAnimator.cs:87-92 (Player/PlayerMoves.cs:18) | `st.IsName("None")` hashes a string for each of 22 players every frame, and SetTrigger uses PlayerMoves.Trigger which builds "T_" + Move by string concat on every call. | Cache the state hash and the trigger hashes in static fields. |  |
| QA-731 | open | S3 | ANI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/MatchOfficials.cs:72 | Each of the 13 officials/coaches calls _g.Evaluate(0f) manually every frame even when culled or far away. | Evaluate only when visible/near the camera, or at a reduced rate for the sideline coaches. |  |
| QA-749 | open | S2 | UI | Performance | UI/MatchHud.cs:242 | ShowPlayCall (called every frame while the call screen is open) calls SetAsLastSibling on the call screen and then on the fade image each frame, which changes sibling order twice per frame and dirties the whole HUD canvas hierarchy (full rebatch every frame). | Only reorder when the sibling index is not already last, or do it once when the screen opens. |  |
| QA-750 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/MatchDirector.cs:1097 | MakeScore allocates a new ScoreSnapshot plus several strings ("crest_"+Id twice, Ordinal, clock, "BALL ON "+SpotText) and MatchHud.SetScore (UI/MatchHud.cs:138) calls PlayClock.ToString(), every frame during pre-snap and live play. | Cache one ScoreSnapshot, build crest ids once and only reformat clock/down text when the values change. |  |
| QA-762 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | UI/MatchHud.cs:401 | UpdateIconGlyph destroys and recreates the glyph children whenever an icon changes button or the scheme flips (Destroy + new GameObjects per receiver), allocating each time receiver slots shuffle. | Pool the Xbox and keycap variants and toggle them. |  |
| QA-769 | open | S2 | UI | Performance | UI/SettingsPanel.cs:85 | Each volume slider tick calls PixSettings.Save (PlayerPrefs.Save), so dragging a slider writes the preferences to disk every frame (also L86-87). | Save on pointer-up / panel close (CloseSettings already saves). |  |
| QA-783 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | UI/PlayCallScreen.cs:164 | UpdateClock runs every frame and allocates `sec.ToString()` plus assigns the TMP text and colour even when the second did not change. | Cache the last second and early-out. |  |
| QA-785 | open | S2 | UI | Performance | UI/PreSnapPanel.cs:61 | Show() runs every frame: SigOf / MenuSig / WheelSig build StringBuilders, string.Join of h.Adjustments.ToArray() (L64) allocates an array and a string, and `hs`/`ms` concatenation allocates again, all per frame during pre-snap. | Compare list contents by version number or hash instead of building strings. |  |
| QA-794 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | UI/PenaltyPanel.cs:65 | Refresh is called every frame by the director and rebuilds every string ("DECIDES IN "+ceil, ToUpperInvariant, quote concatenation, `sig` string) even when nothing changed. | Early-out when the model values are unchanged. |  |
| QA-808 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | UI/FreeRoamHud.cs:109 | Update assigns `SpeedMph.ToString("0.0")` and a concatenated camera/lighting string every frame. | Update only when the displayed value changes. |  |
| QA-830 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Gameplay/Match/MatchInput.cs:65 | Poll allocates three new bool[6] arrays on every call (several callers poll in the same frame), creating garbage 60-120 times per second. | Reuse the arrays as fields. |  |
| QA-855 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Presentation/Overlays/TvUi.cs:26 | Para() generates a new Texture2D per unique (height, slant, lean) key with a 4x4 supersample per pixel on the main thread the first time each card height is shown (comparison card height varies with row count), causing a mid-play hitch; the sprite/texture cache is static and never released. | Pre-warm common heights during loading or use one 9-sliced sprite per slant. |  |
| QA-858 | open | S2 | UI | Performance | Presentation/Replay/ReplayRecorder.cs:166 | The frame pool only gets frames back from trimmed/recycled pre-snap samples (and is capped at 400); every committed clip keeps its frames, so each 1/30 s sample allocates new uint[] / Vector3[] arrays (about 10 KB) and a 10 s play produces about 3 MB of garbage, and trimmed library clips (ReplayData.cs:245) are never returned to the pool, giving GC spikes during live play. | Recycle frames of clips dropped by ReplayLibrary.Trim and pre-allocate a larger pool. |  |
| QA-970 | open | S3 | GFX | Performance | Assets/_Licensed/DraftFever/Props/Field/Lucca Grass Texture/ (Grass_Test_Displacement.exr 136 MB, Normal 45 MB, Albedo 36 MB) | The pitch folder holds assets named Grass_Test_*; the 136 MB EXR displacement imports as a full float texture and is likely unused by any material, inflating import time, Library size and possibly the build. | Delete or exclude unused test textures; confirm references with AssetDatabase.GetDependencies. |  |
| QA-977 | open | S2 | GFX | Performance | Assets/_Project/Settings/PixHDRP.asset (maxDirectionalShadowMapResolution 2048, shadow atlas 4096) | Sun shadows are capped at 2048 px for every tier while night games add 12 spot lights and rim lamps on a 4096 atlas; the shadow budget is not exposed through QualityTiers, so Ultra cannot raise player contact-shadow quality on a 2560x1440 broadcast image. | Expose directional shadow resolution in QualityTiers and set 4096 on High/Ultra. |  |
| QA-989 | open | S3 | GFX | Performance | Assets/_Project/Scenes/Match_Pro.unity (228 MeshRenderers, no LODGroup, no LightProbeGroup) | The crowd is 228 separate static renderers with staticEditorFlags everything, no LOD or cull-distance, and the scene has a single reflection probe and no light probes, so players and the crowd are lit by the sky ambient only (flat) and all 228 crowd draw chunks submit every frame. | Add light probe groups over the pitch/sidelines, per-chunk cull distance, and a probe volume. |  |
| QA-1002 | open | S2 | GFX | Performance | Assets/_Licensed/UI/Logos/CollegeV2/*.png (115 of 138 are 1254 x 1254, others 1145-1374) | The 138 college logos are non power-of-two sprites whose size is not a multiple of 4, so block compression is not possible and each stays an uncompressed RGBA32 (about 6.3 MB each, ~870 MB if all are loaded in a team-select grid); the width/height columns in schools.csv are stored but never used to downscale. | Resample logos to 256/512 power-of-two (multiples of 4) at import and cap maxTextureSize for Logos. |  |
| QA-1022 | open | S2 | UI | Performance | Assets/_Licensed/DraftFever (19 GB) | About 3.09 GB are byte-size duplicates of files > 2 MB (125 duplicate pairs): Characters/Accessories vs Characters/Customization (KneeBrace_Normal 77 MB x2, mouthguard normal 76 MB x2), Packages/StadiumKit vs Props/StadiumProps, Helmets/New/Medium textures vs Helmet Elements Textures/latest base textures, Characters/MainPlayerDelivery vs SkinTextures vs Packages/PlayerMaterials/Tests (T_Arms_Skin_01 x3). | Delete one copy of each and repoint materials; AssetDatabase search confirms references. |  |
| QA-1023 | open | S3 | UI | Performance | Assets/_Licensed/DraftFever/Packages/HairSystem (755 MB) and Characters/Accessories (4.4 GB) | 755 MB of hair cards (fiber_albedo copy.psd 60 MB, alpha.tif 82 MB) is imported though PlayerPrefabBuilder.cs:187 disables every Hair Mesh, and the 8k accessory normals (68-80 MB PNGs) are only clamped to 2048 at import but still stored, imported and hashed in Library. | Move unused packages out of Assets and downsample sources. |  |
| QA-1047 | open | S3 | GFX | Performance | Assets/_Project/Shaders/PixLit/PixLit.shader (1247 lines, 262 multi_compile / shader_feature lines) | PIX/Lit is a full copy of HDRP/Lit (plus three copied HLSL files, 752 lines of properties) pinned to HDRP 17.6 include paths; every material using it compiles the full HDRP Lit variant matrix (decals, SSS, SSR, ray-tracing passes) and any HDRP package update breaks the copy. | Strip unused features with shader_feature_local pruning, add a ShaderVariantCollection/stripper, and keep a diff-from-HDRP note. |  |

BRight : BLeft. |  |

| QA-533 | open | S3 | SIM | Data | Core/DefaultPlaybooks.Defense.cs:90 | GapsFor(5) returns DLeft, BLeft, ARight, BRight, DRight - there is no ALeft, so a five-man rush leaves the left A gap uncovered and the center double-threatened; GapsFor(3) also gives the DT only ALeft. | Use symmetrical gap sets (A both sides for 5 rushers). |  |

If a kick formation orders its slots differently (or a play is flipped/edited), the wrong player snaps/holds/returns, ReturnerUpdate makes the real ReturnerAssignment man 'hold his spot' because `a != _returner`, and the punt rusher at slot 11 gets ReturnerRole. | Resolve roles by Assignment type (KickAssignment, ReturnerAssignment, snap/hold flags) not slot numbers. |  |

| QA-701 | open | S3 | MDL | Models | Gameplay/Player/PlayerLod.cs:54-60 (SimAgentView.cs:214) | The LOD level is chosen with hard thresholds and no hysteresis, refreshed every 4th frame per view: a player standing near 9 m or 24 m (the OL and DL are ~9-12 m from the QB camera) flips between meshes and helmet LODs as the camera moves (visible pop and shadow flicker). | Add +/-10 percent hysteresis and a minimum dwell time per level. |  |
| QA-702 | open | S3 | MDL | Models | Gameplay/Player/PlayerLod.cs:19,62-64,80 (Match/MatchDirector.cs:109) | _level starts at -1 and Apply() only runs from Update(), which does nothing while ViewCamera is null (set only in MatchDirector.Start; free roam never sets it). Until the first update (up to 4 frames after activation, forever in free roam) all helmet/face-mask LOD renderers are active at once, so the helmet meshes overlap and z-fight: another candidate for the helmet 'patch'. | Call Apply(0) in the constructor/OnEnable and set PlayerLod.ViewCamera in FreeRoamGame.Start. |  |

The helmet shell FBX (several sub-meshes: shell, rear plates, hardware) gets New_Helmet_MAT on all slots, so the sub-meshes sample the shell UV mask and baked logo plates; likely the cause of the white patch on the back of helmets. The same loop gives Hands the arms material (no gloves) and Torso+Legs the single uniform material. | Assign materials per slot by name (shell, plate, chrome) and add a model QA check that no helmet sub-mesh shares the shell material by accident. |  |

The player shells are Helmets/New/*/Basic Helmet 1 (basicHelmet_01_GEO.fbx, Basic_Helmet_01_Small/Large.fbx) whose own textures are basicHelmet_01_BaseMap/Normal/MaskMap. If the UV layouts differ, baked logo plates and the mask hole land on the back of the shell. | Overlay each shell's UVs on the texture set; use the shell-specific maps (or switch the prefabs to helmet_LODs.fbx) and render a back-view helmet QA image per size. |  |

Linemen shrink to Medium-sized thin bodies beyond ~11 m: the "super thin waist and legs". | QA-947 | open | S2 | MDL | Models | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/BodyMeshImporter.cs:90-102 | Imported meshes carry only positions, normals, uv0 and weights: tangents are regenerated by RecalculateTangents, vertex colours, uv2 and all blend shapes are dropped (Verify prints the old blendShapes count but Build never copies it). | QA-951 | open | S3 | MDL | Models | Docs/ArtPipeline.md section 5 table (Shoes LOD2 1249 / LOD3 1226 verts; Hair LOD2 = LOD3) | The decimator floors for shoes and hair: LOD3 shoes keep 1226 of 4324 vertices (28 percent, near LOD2) and hair LOD3 equals LOD2, so far LODs of 22 players still carry ~4700 vertices each. | Lower the shoe ratio floor or merge shoes into the legs mesh at LOD3. |  |

| QA-953 | open | S2 | MDL | Models | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/LicensedImportRules.cs:104-123 | Body, head and crowd FBX are imported with default blend-shape settings (importBlendShapes never set to false for Characters/Bodies), so every player carries two 28-key blend shape sets (Head and Eyes) that cost CPU/GPU skinning on 22 players even though no facial animation is driven. | Disable blend shapes on bodies/heads unless a facial system uses them. |  |

| QA-956 | open | S2 | MDL | Models | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/HumanoidMapper.cs:251-253 | When a model has no skinned mesh (animation-only FBX, used.Count == 0) the mapper takes the first existing candidate, which on the 480-bone ARP studio rigs is a control bone, not the deform bone; Docs/AssetInventory.md line 70 states explicit mapping cannot work for these files while HumanoidMapper claims to fix them, so avatars may pass isValid but drive the wrong bones. | Resolve against the deform skeleton only and compare a pose render against the reference avatar. |  |
| QA-1043 | open | S3 | MDL | Models | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/V6ArtBuilder.cs:169-175 (BakedNumbers) | The painted-out jersey numeral rectangles are hard-coded 4096-space pixel boxes for the Medium uniform sheet only; if the licensed uniform sheet is replaced or a Small/Large uniform UV differs by the 15 px legs shift documented in ArtPipeline.md, remnants of the baked "10" stay visible next to the shader numbers. | Detect numerals by threshold per sheet or store boxes next to the texture and validate by diffing. |  |
| QA-1053 | open | S3 | MDL | Models | Assets/_Project/Tests/EditMode/Art/BodyMeshTests.cs:55-75 and Docs/QA/models | LodsKeepTheSilhouetteOfTheBigParts tolerates 10 percent on every bounds axis and 15 percent on surface area per LOD, and LinemenAreVisiblyWiderThanSkillPlayers compares only bounds.size.x; neither measures waist or thigh circumference, so the 8-10 percent leg shrink in test_summary.md (and the 18 percent waist loss of the old Large LOD1) passes. The QA harness has a Medium-only after set. | Add circumference-ratio assertions (waist, hip, thigh, calf >= 0.95 of LOD0) from model_qa_measure data and an after run for Small and Large. |  |

| QA-646 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Gameplay/Player/ActionAnimator.cs:29,72-76,89-98 | The two clip slots are fixed to mixer inputs 1 and 2 and AnimationLayerMixer draws higher inputs ON TOP of lower ones. Every second replacement (stance -> block -> engaged, catch -> tackle) is a hard cut instead of a crossfade. | Always connect the newest clip to the highest input (swap the two inputs' connections when recycling) or blend the two clips in an AnimationMixerPlayable and feed that single node into one layer input. |  |

BoNES retarget takes keep the "root" plus pipe prefix (e.g. the take root-pipe-Ambient_Injury_001_A), so ArtLibrary.ClipByName (exact c.name, ArtLibrary.cs:137-146) fails for short names and the prefix has to be stripped by hand in three places (NormalizeClip, SafeName, report). | Normalize names once at import (strip everything up to the last pipe character) and keep one naming rule. |  |
| QA-912 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:443-447 and 466-476 | Actor role is inferred from a name suffix (_b or _def becomes key_b), but clip_facing.csv shows A is not always the same role: BlockDriveCPass_A faces 180 while BlockDriveCFail_A faces 3; BlockSwimRStale_A faces 6 and its B 170; BlockSpinRStale_A faces 176 and B -22. Offense/defense roles and relative facing differ per pack, so OL can receive the defender half of a take. | Resolve roles per clip from measured heading/ContactTable and store them in the library entry instead of guessing from names. |  |
| QA-913 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:176-189 | ol_pass_set is built from BlockDriveCStale/BlockHitCStale (drive-block stalemate takes that need a partner within arm's length) and ol_stance from OlineCenterPointsAtBlitzC (15.6 s pointing gesture, spread 60 deg) and OlineTransition3ptRToRun*. Played solo, a pass-setting lineman pushes air or points at the defense. | Split solo stances/footwork (bgs_olm_*, ol_run_block) from partnered engagements and never register gestures as stances. |  |
| QA-914 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:203 | zone_drop maps to LinebackerPumpcrowdOne/Two (7 s crowd-pumping gesture, heading spread over 280 deg) and LinebackerTransitionRunFwdTo2pt, so linebackers dropping into zone play a hype-the-crowd animation. | Map zone_drop to backpedal/shuffle takes (bgs_cb_shuffle*, BONES_backpedal_to_shuffle). |  |
| QA-915 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:194 and 292 | catch_dive and catch_jump both resolve to CatchNormalBackCJump (report rows catch_dive / catch_jump), so diving catches play a vertical jump with no layout toward the ground. | Map catch_dive to bgs_dive_ball / DiveShortWithBall / fumble dive takes and keep catch_jump separate. |  |
| QA-916 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:293-294 and 289 | get_up resolves to slowdown_stop_002 (a running stop) and injury_idle to the 51 s Ambient_Injury_001_A take whose heading drifts -91 to 164; fumble resolves to CelebrationFumblerecovery / FrustrationDropInt (celebration and frustration clips). Get-ups, injuries and fumbles use semantically wrong clips. | Build real get-ups with the GetUpBuilder (getup_* rules at lines 46-48) and map fumble to Fumble_Recovery_Dive takes. |  |
| QA-917 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:141 and 291 | qb_snap_shotgun and long_snap register BONES_QB_shotgun_snap_to_fakeHandoff_001 (69 s) and shotgun_to_catch_center_001 (116 s) clips; the snap animation is a fake-handoff plus a 2-minute raw take. | Pick the 1-2 s snap/catch moment via ClipTimingTable and cut it in ClipCleaner. |  |
| QA-918 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Assets/ArtLibraryBuilder.cs:120-121 | turn_left only contains right-turn clips (Jog_to_Jog_135_deg_Right, Trans_Jog_to_Jog_180_deg_Right, hips -36 to -177 deg) while turn_right holds stand-idle and walk turns (5 variants, all loop=1 in clip_facing.csv). Left and right turns come from different families and speeds and the stand-idle turns loop. | Mirror the right turns into _M left turns and build both sides from the same family with loop off. |  |
| QA-919 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Build/AnimatorControllerBuilder.cs:73-75 and 91-93 + clip_facing.csv | The locomotion blend tree uses QuarterbackShuffleBkwdLhand / LeftLhand / RightLhand (left-handed QB shuffles) as backpedal and strafes for all 22 players; measured hips headings are -65, -147 and -129 deg, i.e. the body is twisted 60-150 deg from its movement direction when backpedaling or strafing. | Use facing-corrected neutral shuffle loops (slow_shuffle_loop_*, bgs_cb_shuffle*) and bake the offset. |  |
| QA-920 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Build/AnimatorControllerBuilder.cs:86-97 | The Freeform blend tree has children only on the Z axis and the strafe axis plus a walk entry that is the jog clip at timeScale 0.5 (walk key is missing); the "diagonal fillers" comment at line 94 has no code. Diagonal runs blend a strafe with a run and skate. | Add forward-left/right and back-left/right children from bgs_* diagonals and use a real walk cycle after the library rebuild. |  |
| QA-922 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ClipCleaner.cs:78 and 143 | PoseSeq.FromClip is capped at min(length, 60) s and MeasureHeading at 20 s, but BoNES takes run to 206 s (standing_idle_loop_2_001), 116 s and 187 s; the tails are truncated silently and the heading/spread of long takes is measured on the first 20 s only, so FaceForward can rotate by the wrong angle. | Sample in segments, report truncation in Result.note, and clamp only with an explicit plan field. |  |
| QA-923 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ClipCleaner.cs:210-244 | Simplify runs a Ramer-Douglas-Peucker pass on every curve independently (tolerance 0.004), including the root quaternion components, then overwrites all tangents with central differences. Independently reduced quaternion components denormalize root rotation and the authored smooth tangents are lost, giving pops at loop seams. | Simplify muscle curves only, keep root Q/T at full rate, preserve tangents. |  |
| QA-924 | open | S3 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ClipCleaner.cs:67 | Two-actor detection uses the regex (_a or _b) at the end of the name, so any solo clip named like "idle_a" or "run_b" is treated as paired (no AutoTrim, no FaceForward) and keeps its T-pose lead-in. | Pair detection should come from the pack (Motus A/B, BoNES retarget) not a suffix. |  |
| QA-926 | open | S2 | STN | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Gameplay/Player/StanceClips.cs:20-21 | Stance loops over 8 s are windowed with a hard-coded 2.8 s trim at both ends (assuming a 2.8 s T-pose calibration), while the stance takes in clip_facing.csv are 21-39 s with spread; the stagger start uses (seed*0.37) % 1 which is negative for negative seeds. A different calibration length shows a T-pose flash on snap. | Bake the trim per clip in ClipCleaner (Result.t0/t1) and store it in ClipTimingTable; use Mathf.Repeat for the stagger. |  |
| QA-927 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Docs/QA/stance_gallery/clip_facing.csv (loop column) + AnimationImportSettings.cs:48-55 | Many clips with clearly one-shot content are looping in the library: stance_to_sprint (6 variants loop=1), turn_right stand-idle turns (loop=1), DlineTransition3ptRToRunFwd0deg (loop=1), shuffle_to_sprint (loop=1). Gameplay trimming (ClipPlan) masks it for some actions but not for the animator controller states. | Fix import flags as above, then re-run ClipFacingAudit so the CSV reflects the new flags. |  |
| QA-928 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Tools/Blender/retarget_actions.py:86-100 | Retargeting copies WORLD location and rotation of every bone (COPY_LOCATION + COPY_ROTATION in WORLD space) with one uniform armature scale from hip height (hipB/hipA). Limb-length differences are not solved, so hands miss the ball, feet float/penetrate, and fingers/spine are over-constrained: a major source of the "mocap needs cleanup" complaint. | Retarget rotations only (copy rotation in local/pose space) for limbs, keep location only for hips/root, add foot IK pinning on contact frames. |  |
| QA-929 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Tools/Blender/retarget_actions.py:128-129 | `if os.path.exists(out_path): continue` silently keeps stale baked FBX when the script is re-run with new parameters (no --force flag), so fixed retargets are never applied and old broken takes keep importing. | Add a --force option and log skipped outputs. |  |
| QA-930 | open | S3 | ANI | Animation | Tools/Blender/retarget_actions.py:114-119 | For files with a single non-Key action the take is renamed to the FBX file name; multi-take files that contain exactly one non-Key action lose their real action name, and Key-prefixed duplicate actions are skipped by the seen set, so the Key variant (often the real animation) can be dropped. | Name by action and keep a mapping table in the output folder. |  |
| QA-931 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Tools/Blender/retarget_actions.py:132-139 | Export uses use_armature_deform_only=False and bake_anim_use_all_bones=True: control bones are baked and exported, and Humanoid avatars of the output FBX map the control rig (Docs/AssetInventory.md line 70 already notes automap failures), producing the invalid avatars HumanoidMapper.FixAllInvalid has to repair. | Export deform bones only after retarget and verify with the avatar report. |  |
| QA-1030 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Tools/Blender/model_qa_measure.py and Docs/QA/models/before_measurements.csv (yaw column) | The pose measurements record per-pose body yaw straight from the clips: idle -90, stance_2pt 0, stance_3pt -0.6, dl_stance +139, ol_stance -91, run -93, tackle_wrap +106, tackle_big_hit -124. The 230-degree spread between stance clips proves the facing problem is in the animation data and not in the sim, and no corrected-yaw QA run exists. | Re-run after the facing/clean pass and gate on yaw within +-10 degrees for all stance and locomotion keys. |  |
| QA-1031 | open | S1 | STN | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Art/ClipFacingTable.cs:42-65 and Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ClipCleaner.cs:110-117 | The facing table is keyed only by clip.name and holds the raw-clip heading. ClipCleaner FaceForward rotates the cleaned clip to +Z and the library replaces same-named raw clips with the Clean copy (ArtLibraryBuilder clean-dir merge), so the stale table entry still applies its full correction to an already-corrected clip: the double correction the class summary warns about, with nothing (hash, length, version) to detect staleness. After a cleaning pass every corrected stance would face backwards again. | Store a clip fingerprint (length, first-frame heading, version) in each Entry and ignore mismatches; rebuild the table automatically at the end of ClipCleaner/ArtLibraryBuilder runs. |  |
| QA-1032 | open | S2 | STN | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Art/ClipFacingTable.cs:17-20 and Docs/QA/stance_gallery/clip_facing.csv | Heading is sampled at only three instants (hips0/1/2 at start, middle, end) and a clip is left uncorrected when spread exceeds 70 deg or the yaw is under 10 deg; a stance that wanders (T-pose calibration, turns: BONES stances show shoulders -46 to -136) or a cut that returns to its start heading looks stable and is mis-corrected; MinCorrection 10 leaves 9-degree residual errors that line a 5-man OL up visibly crooked. | Sample at 10+ points per clip, correct on the frame range actually played, and reduce MinCorrection to 3 deg for stances. |  |
| QA-1045 | open | S2 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ContactClipBuilder.cs:109-120 | Run flips every pair take's importer between Original (SetOriginalPositions(true)) and Center of Mass (false) with no try/finally, unlike Sheet() which guards it; an exception between the two passes (e.g. a missing clip in lib.ClipByName) leaves ~224 tackle/block FBX imported at their authored world offsets and tackles play with large position offsets until Restore is run by hand. Each flip also SaveAndReimports every pair FBX twice per run. | Wrap pass 1 in try/finally that restores the project default and skip reimports when the value already matches. |  |
| QA-1050 | open | S3 | ANI | Animation | Docs/QA/stance_gallery/clip_facing.csv (header) vs Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/ClipFacingAudit.cs:75 | The committed audit CSV has the old columns (hips0..shoulders2, upright) while the tool now writes headY, meanYaw, spread, offset, handDown and footBack, and the runtime table was rebuilt at 09:31; the CSV was not regenerated, so it no longer shows the offsets actually applied in game. | Regenerate the CSV with the table and add the build time to its first line. |  |
| QA-1054 | open | S3 | ANI | Animation | Assets/_Project/Scripts/Editor/Tools/AnimTools.cs:23 and CycleSpeeds.cs | CycleSpeeds writes Logs/cycle_speeds.txt only for 14 hard-coded clip names, and AnimTools.AuditOnly has no summary assertion, so speed ladder mismatches (jog/run/sprint natural speeds feeding clipSpeeds) are never checked against LocoSpeed after the clips are cleaned. | Compute natural speeds for every locomotion key and fail when the ladder is not monotonic. |  |

| QA-029 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | match_presnap_1.png, match_presnap_2.png | Default match camera is a high sideline broadcast camera (about 30 m up, pitched ~35 deg) so players are ~60 px tall at 1440p; the line of scrimmage runs vertically across the screen. The user wants a Madden/2K8 behind-QB camera (ref 1,3,4,25) filling the frame with the formation. | Make the behind-QB camera the default for user-controlled plays; keep this one for CPU-vs-CPU. |  |
| QA-124 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | bc_manual_behind_qb.png | 'Behind QB' replay preset sits ~2 m behind the QB at ground level so the QB fills the frame and the action is occluded; 2K8 refs elevate the camera 6-9 m. | Raise this preset to ~7 m height, 10 m back, pitched 28 deg. |  |
| QA-127 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | bc_fp_defense_presnap.png | First-person camera shows a huge skin-coloured hand/forearm blob (lower-left) and a blue triangular shard (lower-middle) of the player's own mesh clipping into view, plus grey facemask bars. | Hide own arms in FP, fix near clip plane and the facemask overlay. |  |
| QA-128 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | bc_fp_defense_presnap.png | FP camera sits at ground level pitched to the grass line so most of the frame is turf and the offense is a thin strip. | Tie FP pitch to stance height or lift the eye point. |  |
| QA-144 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | bc_gallery_combined.png | Broadcast camera at the goal line looks across at a high oblique angle: far half of the field is cut off and the goalpost leans (perspective not corrected). | Level the horizon and use a narrower FOV. |  |
| QA-202 | open | S1 | CAM | Camera | Logs/autotest_bc6/bc_fp_offense_live.png | First-person live frame: a giant skin-coloured forearm/hand (the player's own pointing arm) fills the right 40 percent of the screen with a visible cut-off cylinder end, and a grey circular helmet/facemask ring crosses the lower frame; the view is dominated by the player's own mesh. | Hide the own-body arms in FP (or render with a separate near camera/FOV) and fix the facemask overlay sizing. |  |
| QA-204 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | bc_fp_offense_live.png | FP camera is at the helmet but the pitch/horizon is rolled (the stadium roof lines slope ~10 degrees left to right) and the goal posts are 25 yards away yet the camera shows the pre-snap line 3 m away from the view origin; horizon tilt from head bob is excessive. | Clamp roll to 1-2 degrees and bob amplitude to 1 cm. |  |
| QA-205 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | bc_manual_high.png | The HIGH replay angle is a near-vertical top-down view (looking straight down at ~85 degrees) at a skewed yaw (yard lines run diagonally across the frame, numerals rotated 60 degrees), making the formation unreadable; all players look like foreshortened discs. | Limit pitch to 55-60 degrees and align yaw to the yard lines. |  |

| QA-233 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | match_kick_close_1.png | Close-up camera is a side-on 35mm cinematic cut at 2:00 when the clock has not yet started: it is on the kicker's teammate rather than the kicker/ball; the referee at right is cut by the board; the 30-yard marker is tiny. | Cut to the ball/kicker, not an arbitrary player. |  |

| QA-283 | open | S1 | CAM | Camera | Reference 2K8 4.webp, 1, 3, 6, 7 vs run3/match_presnap_1.png | 2K8 reference (user bar): the camera sits behind the offense at ~8 m height, the formation fills the frame width, linemen are ~100 px tall at 400 px frame height and QB/RB sit in the lower third. Our v0.5 gameplay camera is a high sideline broadcast shot where players are ~60 px tall at 1440p with the LOS running vertically; the screen is 70 percent empty turf. | Default user play to a Classic (2K8) behind-the-QB camera 8 m up / 10 m back, pitch ~30 deg, FOV ~38 deg. |  |
| QA-298 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | 2K8 ref 6, 19 | After the snap 2K8's camera pulls back/up slightly and tracks the ball carrier from behind; ours stays on a fixed sideline broadcast angle in v0.5 and cuts to tight close-ups after the whistle that hide the pile (bc_shot_tight_carrier.png). | Implement the pull-back and follow-from-behind rule for user plays. |  |
| QA-680 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/UserCameraMath.cs:76,112 (MatchCameraRig.cs:72) | The new user camera is a plain transform with no collision or occlusion handling, and the only clamp is z in [MinZ-14, MaxZ+14] = +/-76 m although the back of the end zone is at +/-55 m: with the ball inside the red zone (camera 14.5 m behind the LOS plus the 3 m snap pull-back and 8.4 m height) the lens ends up behind the end-zone wall, inside stands/LED boards or outside the stadium. | Clamp the camera to the real playing-area bounds (end line + 6 m, sideline + 10 m, measured from FieldGeometry) and pull toward the focus (reduce Back) instead of letting it leave the pitch; add a light occlusion cast against the stadium layer. |  |
| QA-681 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:195-198,265 | First-person and any non-Behind mode still derive MoveBasisYaw from _behindYaw, which follows the player's velocity (targetYaw = Atan2(_userVel)): a held diagonal stick turns the camera, which turns the stick basis again (feedback = running in circles), and a backpedaling defender (speed > 1.5 m/s) swings the first-person camera 180 degrees to look at his own end zone while he faces the play. | Base first-person yaw on the body facing (or Frame.DownfieldYaw for offense) rather than velocity, and use a fixed downfield basis for stick input. |  |
| QA-683 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:102 (Match/MatchDirector.cs:135,346,460,569) | Cycle() still walks Broadcast -> Behind -> FirstPerson during user play, so a View press switches the user to the sideline TV camera (the note-3 bad case) and DefaultCamera persists it for the next game. | Exclude Broadcast from the user-play cycle (only Behind/FirstPerson) and keep sim games on Broadcast only. |  |
| QA-684 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:62,119 | Broadcast offset 44 m / 17 m and FOV 27 are hard coded with no relation to the stadium bowl: depending on the lower deck the lens can sit inside seating structure, and FOV 27 frames only ~21 m vertically so deep passes and wide formations leave the frame. | Expose the offset/FOV in a CameraProfile asset, verify against the bowl bounds and widen FOV with pass distance. |  |
| QA-685 | open | S2 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:115-121 (Look/WeatherSystem.cs:149) | The broadcast camera swaps to the opposite sideline every time the offense changes direction (every possession and quarter) while the flood-lamp rig is fixed: 'Flood_E' lamps are biased as the rim light seen from the broadcast side, true only when the camera is on +X, so half the plays have rim and key reversed; coaches stay on fixed sides. | Keep the broadcast camera on one sideline or bias the lamps per play from Cams.Frame.TowardsPlusZ. |  |
| QA-686 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/MatchCameraRig.cs:12,203-231 | With the new [DefaultExecutionOrder(-200)] the rig runs before BodyMotion lean/foot IK (order 0), BallFollower (150) and RagdollRig (200), so the first-person anchor reads the head bone before any of them pose it: one frame lag/jitter, and a ragdolled first-person player's camera does not follow his head. | Keep the Behind/Broadcast proxies at -200 but update the first-person anchor in a separate LateUpdate with an order after RagdollRig. |  |
| QA-687 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Cameras/UserCameraMath.cs:110 | `if (c.Defense) { }` is an empty branch: the defense camera has no tuning of its own (it reuses the offense preset mirrored), so behind the defense the secondary, not the line, is the foreground and the pass rush is hard to read. | Add a defense preset (lower, closer to the LOS) or remove the flag. |  |
| QA-688 | open | S3 | CAM | Camera | Gameplay/Match/CollisionFeel.cs:27,111-113 | Camera shake adds noise to the camera transform every LateUpdate but never restores it (_appliedPos/_appliedRot/_applied are write-only); it works only because CinemachineBrain rewrites the pose each frame, so when the Brain is disabled (SetOverride for replays, AutoTest Aim) a manually placed camera accumulates a random walk. | Subtract the stored offset at the start of the next LateUpdate or skip shake while Cams.Overridden. |  |

## 5. Engine / AI / Rules (226)

| ID | Status | Sev | Track | Category | Where | Issue | Fix | Verified by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| QA-012 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | autotest.txt free roam (run1) | Free-roam player hit "max speed 26.0 mph" in a 50 m sprint; real elite football top speed is ~22 mph and the HUD shows 18.4 mph mid-run, so the speed model is much too fast for the 'realistic speed' goal (All-Pro 2K8 bar). | Cap sprint at ~21-22 mph with acceleration-limited ramp (0-15 mph in ~2 s). |  |
| QA-058 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | run3/boxscore.txt | Third autopilot game: 25 snaps, 3 touchdowns, 4 sacks, 0 INT, 0 fumbles, 1 penalty; HOME yards 40 (pass 29, rush 11) and AWAY rush 3 yards on the whole game; running plays gain 0-4 yards every time and the passing game is all-or-nothing (sacks or 30-yard completions). | Tune run blocking/holes and the completion-yardage distribution. |  |
| QA-137 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | bc_halftime_stats.png | Buffalo first half: 14 total yards, 0 rushing yards on 2 carries, 0 first downs, 3rd down 0/1, yet 3 points; Miami 80 yards; tiny play counts per half and extreme sack frequency. | Check clock burn per play and sack probability. |  |
| QA-164 | open | S2 | SIM | Rules | v030_penalty_panel_accept.png | Defensive offside (dead-ball foul) offers ACCEPT/DECLINE with "DECLINE - NO PLAY: REPLAY THE DOWN"; dead-ball pre-snap fouls are enforced automatically and cannot be declined, and the decline text makes no sense when no play was run. | Auto-enforce dead-ball fouls; only live-ball fouls get the choice. |  |
| QA-180 | open | S1 | SIM | Engine/AI | Logs/autotest/boxscore.txt, autotest_full/boxscore.txt, autotest_match1/boxscore.txt | Three full autopilot games end 6-0, 6-0 and 6-7 with 24-29 snaps, total yards of 33/-11, 3/-6 and 6/94, 0-2 first downs, 0 interceptions and 0 fumbles in all three games, 2 FGs and 0 TDs: scoring, yardage and turnovers are not remotely football-like. | Rebalance pass completion, run yards, pass rush and add turnovers (target ~330 yds, ~20 first downs, 1.2 turnovers per team per game). |  |
| QA-181 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | autotest/boxscore.txt | Box score header "passes 2" for 24 snaps while the play log lists 4+ INCOMPLETE plays; the pass counter is wrong or passes complete almost never (2 completions in a game). | Count attempts in the box score; raise completion probability for open receivers. |  |
| QA-182 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | autotest/boxscore.txt (sacks 4 in 24 snaps) | Home 4 sacks in 24 snaps (17 percent of snaps), Away -11 yards: pass rush wins on nearly every dropback and 'sack' also ends plays that should be throw-aways. | Lower sack probability to ~6 percent of dropbacks; add QB throw-away/scramble. |  |
| QA-183 | open | S3 | SIM | Rules | autotest_full/boxscore.txt | After a 6-0 TD in Q2 the CPU/autopilot attempts a TWO-POINT TRY (failed) rather than the extra point; early-game 2-point tries are unrealistic. | Restrict 2-pt tries to situations by the chart (late game deficits). |  |
| QA-185 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | Logs/autotest_bc6/autotest.txt (clips) | 7 of the 12 best-play clips of one game are "BIG HIT score 60" and 3 are SACK: big hits trigger far too often (the big-hit ragdoll/contact fires on routine tackles). | Raise the big-hit energy threshold; limit to ~1 per quarter. |  |
| QA-266 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | run3/match_pass_2.png | "3RD & 21, BALL ON MIA 9" for the BUF offense at 0:21 of the 2nd quarter: the offense lost 12 net yards on the drive's first two downs (sacks/negative runs) - such repeated negative plays occur in all three autotest games. | Check sack rate and negative-run frequency in the sim. |  |
| QA-273 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | Logs/qa_audit/run3/Player.log (autotest milestones) | Defensive play-call variety is broken: in the third quarter the CPU defense called "Cover 3 Sky" on 5 of 6 consecutive snaps (Smash, HB Screen, Quick Hitches, WR Screen, Jet Sweep Right all vs Cover 3 Sky) and the Q2 drive was Cover 3 Sky twice in a row; real defenses mix looks by down/distance. | Add a call-history penalty and down/distance/formation weighting to the CPU defensive caller. |  |
| QA-274 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | run3/Player.log | The autopilot/CPU offense repeats the same play: "Jet Sweep Right" is called in Q1, then twice more in Q3 (and again vs a Cover 3 Sky), plus "Smash" and "Double Outs" twice; the play history does not suppress repeats and the CPU runs a Jet Sweep on 2nd & 7 and 2nd & 8. | Keep a recent-calls list and a per-situation weight table. |  |
| QA-275 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | run3/Player.log | 1st & goal at the 6 (staged) the offense runs Inside Zone Left and scores in 3 s (clock 2:00 -> 1:57); a 6-yard TD in 3 game-seconds is the whole play. Plays burn the clock inconsistently: Q1 plays took 23 s, 28 s, 31 s, 28 s while the Q3 plays took 4 s (3rd & 3 incomplete at 1:26 -> 1:22) and 7 s. | Model play duration from the play type plus the time between plays with the clock rules. |  |
| QA-276 | open | S2 | SIM | Rules | run3/Player.log | After the first TD the state line reads "BUF ball 1&15 @85" and the team immediately attempts a two-point try ("Toss Sweep Right vs Goal Line Gap Control") with no extra-point option chosen by the user; the autopilot/CPU goes for two after the first score of the game, which is not realistic. | Choose PAT vs 2-pt by the coach chart; default PAT. |  |
| QA-277 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | run3/Player.log | Two consecutive sacks (Q2: 2nd & 16 -> SACK at 1:16, 3rd & 21 -> SACK at 0:49) pushed MIA from its own 20 to its own 9 (3 sacks cost 16 yards in a row) and the third-down call at 3rd & 21 was Corner-Post vs Cover 4: offense gets sacked on both consecutive deep dropbacks. | Lower sack rate and force a throw-away or scramble when the pocket collapses. |  |
| QA-278 | open | S3 | SIM | Rules | run3/Player.log | Kickoff outcomes disagree: Q1 kickoff = TOUCHBACK with the ball at the 30, Q2 kickoff ends at the 20 ("MIA ball 1&10 @20"), Q3 kickoff "@30" with the result tag KICKOFF rather than RETURN; touchback spot differs from the kick return spot with no return yardage shown. | Use rule-set touchback spot and report return yards. |  |
| QA-291 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | 2K8 reference (realistic speed) vs run1 autotest | The user asks for realistic game speed: our free-roam sprint peaks at 26 mph (autotest), accelerates almost instantly (0 to 18 mph in about 1 s) and plays take 3-30 s inconsistently; football top speed is ~21-22 mph with 2-3 s acceleration. | Cap the speed/acceleration curves and add the play-duration model. |  |
| QA-311 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:398 | The Snap event hard-codes AgentA 0 for kickoff, 1 for punt and 4 for everything else; slot 4 is assumed to be the center but a formation with a different slot order (flipped, empty set, TE inline) will name the wrong snapper; the same Agents[4] assumption is used in PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:221 and :227 for bad snaps. | Find the snapper once (the OL slot with Position.C, or the Specials snapper) and store SnapperId. |  |
| QA-312 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:626-679 | Pre-snap penalty rolls also run for punts and field goals using Agents[2..6] awareness and Agents[10] as the culprit; on a punt formation those slots are not OL, so FalseStart/IllegalFormation names random players. Offside/Encroachment always blames Agents[11] and Agents[12] even if those slots are CBs or safeties that cannot jump offside. | Pick the culprit from the actual line (DL/OL slots near the LOS) for the current formation, or skip when none exists. |  |
| QA-313 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:642-645 | The false-start culprit is always the lineman with the lowest Awareness, so the same player draws every flag and a uniform-rated line always blames slot 2. | Weight a random pick by (1 - awareness) with Rng. |  |
| QA-314 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:446-454 | ForceEnd at MaxPlaySeconds=22 whistles the play dead mid-run with EndReason.TimeLimit, and the result is built as an ordinary run/pass from wherever the carrier is; a kickoff return or long scramble that exceeds 22 s is cut off in open field with no tackle animation. | Convert to a tackle (carrier goes Down) or extend the limit for Kind != Scrimmage. |  |
| QA-315 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:456-481 | SeparateBodies skips every pair if either body is fallen, so players walk straight through downed players and a tackled carrier is non-solid (no tripping over bodies, no pile). | Keep a reduced-radius collision with fallen bodies, or apply a stumble when running over one. |  |
| QA-316 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:469 | The QB<->ball-carrier ghost exemption is applied for the whole play: a back who has the ball and a same-side agent in slot 0 pass through each other forever, including a QB trailing as a blocker on a reverse or screen. | Limit the exemption to the first ~0.8 s after Handoff (time-stamp Blocks.HandedOff). |  |
| QA-319 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:270 | ClampInField clamps Y to [-9,109]; at a ball spot inside the 3 yard line the punter and shotgun QB are clamped (punter 15 yards back from the own 3 is forced to Y=-9, a 12 yard snap) and several deep players share the same Y so protectors and returners overlap. | Shift the whole formation as a unit instead of clamping individual slots. |  |
| QA-320 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.cs:270 | X is clamped to 0.6 which collapses two receivers placed wider than the sideline (hash at 23.6 plus a -24 split) onto the same X; they then get pushed apart by SeparateBodies at the snap. | Clamp relative to the sideline per slot and nudge the next slot inward instead. |  |
| QA-321 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:121-129 | A fumble that rolls out the back of the opponent end zone (Y>100 then end line) or the side line in the end zone is treated like a generic OutOfBounds with the offense keeping the ball; it should be a touchback, and out the back of the offense's own end zone a safety. Only the sideline is right. | In UpdateLooseBall detect end-line crossings and award touchback/safety based on who last had the ball. |  |
| QA-322 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:167-182 | In Recover() any time an opponent is within 3.8 yards the recovering player always falls on the ball (scoop is only possible with no opponent near), and the play is whistled at once; there is no dog-pile, no second scramble and no strip-fumble-recovered-and-run when defenders are close. | Add a short contested window (0.3-0.6 s) with re-recovery chances before whistling. |  |
| QA-323 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:106 | Fumble() resets a Down carrier to AgentState.Normal even though Body.FallenTime>0 and the tackler was just set to Tackling; the carrier is "up" in state terms and can be re-tackled or pick the ball back up while lying on the ground. | Leave State=Down and exclude Down carriers explicitly in the recovery loop. |  |
| QA-324 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:138-149 | Loose-ball recovery adds Rng.NextFloat()*0.35 to a score whose skill range is only about 0.2-0.9, so a fumble is won almost randomly (an OL can beat a DB), and 55% of hot balls (speed>7) are simply skipped each tick so nobody picks up a ball at their feet for several frames. | Reduce the noise term to ~0.1 and drop the speed>7 skip after the first bounce. |  |
| QA-325 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:203 | A pitch that hits the ground is logged as a Fumble (Fumbled, FumbleCount++) even though a pitch is a legal backward pass and nobody had possession; recovery by the defense then scores as FumbleLost with the wrong credit. | Treat it as a loose ball without a fumble credit unless the intended receiver had possession. |  |
| QA-326 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:230-243 | An incomplete pass is ended on first ground contact and Ball.Kill() zeroes the velocity at once, so the ball stops dead on the grass with no bounce/roll; a throw that lands out of bounds and one in bounds look the same. | Let the ball bounce for ~0.3 s before Kill. |  |
| QA-327 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Ball.cs:255-264 | Out of bounds for a runner is tested on body centre Pos.X only while the touchdown test uses the ball (Pos.Y+0.25, +0.8 on a dive), so the sideline and the goal line use different rules; a carrier whose body is in but arm/ball over the line keeps going. | Test OOB with the ball position or body +/- radius, consistently with the TD rule. |  |
| QA-329 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Result.cs:86-91 + TackleResolver.cs:228-245 | A strip-sack never logs a Sack event or sets SackerId/QbSack because the fumble branch returns before the sack bookkeeping; the sack is lost from stats and the result reads as a plain fumble recovery. | Set QbSack/SackerId and log the Sack event before the fumble roll. |  |
| QA-330 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Result.cs:30,94 | Elapsed time is hard-coded as Time+1.0 for scrimmage plays and Max(3, Time+0.8) for incompletions, so a 1.5 s incompletion burns 3 s while a 12 s scramble burns 13 s; no relation to the actual stopping reason. | Compute elapsed from Time plus a documented constant per ending reason. |  |
| QA-331 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.Result.cs:66 | TD yards are computed from State.BallSpot rather than LosY, so after a two-point/PAT override (PlaySimulation.cs:167-168) or a changed spot the credited yardage is inconsistent with the field position used by the sim. | Use LosY. |  |
| QA-333 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:118-125 | Motion moves at a flat 3.4 yd/s for at most 16 yards but AutoSnap fires at 0.6 s (PlaySimulation.cs:22), so with AutoSnap on a motion man travels ~2 yards and is snapped mid-stride; there is no rule that the man must be set or only moving laterally/backward at the snap. | Do not snap while the man is moving faster than a walk, or require motion to finish before AutoSnap. |  |
| QA-334 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:191 | LB shift In uses y=Math.Max(1.4, y-1.3), which moves linebackers who are already at y<1.4 (e.g. a wide-9 OLB at 1.0) backward by 0.4. | Use Math.Min(y, ...) semantics: only decrease y, never increase. |  |
| QA-335 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | PlaySimulation.PreSnap.cs:98-103 | CanMotion refuses any receiver whose Y>-0.75, i.e. slot WR/TE on the line cannot go in motion, but a back directly behind the QB at Y=-1 can; there is no check that the motion does not put fewer than 7 men on the line or that the man is not an OL. | Validate the post-motion formation (7 on line, one in motion). |  |
| QA-337 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | AgentBody.cs:229 | The traction-limit scaling only runs when at>0, so braking (at<0) combined with a full lateral term is never limited and the body can change direction instantly in a hard cut-and-stop, giving the "too sharp" jukes and defenders who stop on a dime. | Apply the friction-circle clamp to both signs of at. |  |
| QA-338 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | AgentBody.cs:105-106 | BackpedalFactor 0.50-0.64 and ShuffleFactor 0.68-0.82 of VMax (about 6-7.7 and 8.2-9.8 yd/s) let a CB backpedal at 7 yd/s and shuffle sideways at 9 yd/s; real backpedal is ~5-6 yd/s and lateral shuffle ~6-7, so DBs stay too far ahead of WRs and the defense is too hard to beat deep. | Reduce Backpedal to 0.42-0.55 and Shuffle to 0.55-0.68. |  |
| QA-339 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | AgentBody.cs:79 + PlaySimulation.cs:237 | AgentBody is rebuilt every play with Energy=1, so fatigue never carries across plays or drives; a DL who has rushed 12 snaps runs as fast as at the start. | Persist Energy per Player and recover during the huddle runoff. |  |
| QA-341 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | AgentBody.cs:157-169 | A fallen body keeps full Pos advance with Vel decaying at 9 yd/s^2 and never rotates facing; tackled players slide several yards (a 7 yd/s carrier slides ~2.7 yards after tackle) with the previous heading and no turn to face the ground. | Cap the slide, damp stronger when Down, and expose a FallDir for the animator. |  |
| QA-342 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | AgentBody.cs:93 | Radius is clamped to 0.34-0.46 yards from mass; a 330 lb OL (radius 0.46 -> 0.84 m shoulder) and a 175 lb DB (0.34 -> 0.62 m) differ by little, so OL interior gaps appear tight and DBs look wider than real. | Scale radius with mass more strongly (0.30 to 0.50) or use an elliptical shape facing the heading. |  |
| QA-343 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | BallSim.cs:251-258 | Bounce restitution 0.46+-0.09 and horizontal scale 0.74+-0.12 mean a punt or fumble bounces like a basketball every time; there is no angle of incidence logic (prolate ball) beyond a random yaw, so loose balls roll predictably straight. | Add an orientation-dependent restitution and sideways kick. |  |
| QA-344 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:43-63 | Tackle attempts only look at distance to the carrier; the defender's facing is never tested, so a DB running away or an LB facing backwards makes a full-strength tackle at reach 1.3 yards. The tackle category (head-on/behind) uses only the carrier's direction. | Penalize margin or skip the attempt when the defender faces more than ~100 degrees away from the carrier. |  |
| QA-345 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:106,113-149 | Margin = skill diff + mod + Gaussian(0,12) + 7.5; with equal ratings the clean-stop chance is about 88% and broken-tackle only 2%, so almost every contact ends in an immediate whistle. Open-field one-on-ones never produce NFL-like missed tackles (~15-25%) for good ball carriers against average tacklers. | Retune the constant 7.5 downward (about 0) and raise the broken-tackle weight for speed/elusiveness; calibrate against the batch tests. |  |
| QA-346 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:174 | The push-out-of-bounds branch also fires for QB sacks and scrambles near the sideline (type becomes PushOutOfBounds so FinishTackle ends at the out-of-bounds branch before the sack branch), so a QB hit while in the pocket at X<1.3 is credited as OOB, not a sack. | Skip push-out when sack is true or the carrier is still behind the LOS in the pocket. |  |
| QA-347 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:217-220 | PushOutOfBounds teleports the carrier's Body.Pos to X=-0.15 or Width+0.15 in one frame (up to 1.3 yards of pop) and zeroes forward velocity; visible snap on screen. | Apply a velocity impulse and let the body cross the line over a few frames. |  |
| QA-349 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:246-251 | The safety test in a tackle uses body Pos.Y<=0.1 while the spot (EndY) is Pos.Y+0.2, so a runner whose ball is a yard in the field of play can be ruled a safety (and conversely a carrier stopped at Y=0.05 with the ball at 0.25 is spotted at the 0 but called safety). | Use the ball/EndY position for the safety rule. |  |
| QA-350 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:368,379 | c.IgnoreAgent holds a single defender id; a second shoestring/broken tackle overwrites the first, so the defender who just whiffed can immediately re-attempt once another defender touches the carrier. | Use a small per-carrier ignore set with timestamps. |  |
| QA-351 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:181-190 | Fumble probability per tackle is 0.4% (wrap) up to 3% (big hit), times a 0.7 fudge; with ~45 tackles per team per game this yields about 0.15 fumbles per game from tackles vs NFL ~0.5-0.6 lost fumbles per team, so ball security is too good and strip plays never happen. | Raise the base to ~0.012 and add a dedicated strip attempt by the tackler's Tackling/Strength rating. |  |
| QA-352 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:259-271 | MaybeFoul draws a RoughingThePasser at 0.40*dis (about 30%) for any tackle on the QB after the throw, even on a ball already caught 30 yards away, and has no clock window (late hit vs simultaneous release). | Only roll when the hit occurs more than ~0.3 s after release and within a few yards of the passer. |  |
| QA-353 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:330 | pFree is multiplied by Dt*60*0.1 which is always 0.1 (Dt is a constant 1/60); the extra factor is meaningless and the break-free chance is frame-rate coupled to the number of calls. | Define the rate per second and multiply by dt. |  |
| QA-354 | open | S3 | SIM | Engine/AI | TackleResolver.cs:324-325 | Draggers aim Want at c.Pos - c.Vel.Norm()*(radii-0.05) and multiply by 8; when c.Vel is zero Norm() is zero, collapsing the hold point onto the carrier centre and producing an unbounded force that the body clamps to max speed (jitter/overlap). | Fall back to the dragger's own direction when carrier speed is near zero. |  |
| QA-355 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | CatchResolver.cs:132-148 | Engages() rolls once per ball for each defender and, on failure, locks him out of the pass permanently (_lock = float.MaxValue); a DB within reach of the arrival point can be blind (28% of the time for DBs, 70-90% for linemen) for the entire flight even if the ball goes right to him later. | Re-roll each time the ball re-enters reach (e.g. every 0.25 s) rather than a permanent lock. |  |
| QA-356 | open | S2 | SIM | Engine/AI | CatchResolver.cs:112-122 | DPI base probability 0.13 + 0.0125*air yards, scaled up to 0.22 cap, is rolled on every pass whose receiver has a defender within 2.1 yards; short throws with trailing defenders flag around 5-22% so DPI would occur on ~8-10% of attempts vs ~1.5-2% in the NFL. | Reduce base to ~0.02 and scale by air yards, trailing and contact time. |  |
